\int_{s}^{t}p_{k}=\frac{p_{k-1}(t_{k})}{p_{k-1}(s_{k})}\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}+\int_{s_{k}}^{t_{k}}p_{k-1}(t_{k})\,dz+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}p_{k-1}\geq\int_{s}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}+\int_{s_{k}}^{t_{k}}p_{k-1}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}p_{k-1}=\int_{s}^{t}p_{k-1}.

Suppose instead that
a_{k}>0
. Taking
s=-\infty
and
t=s_{k}
in part
(d)
of the inductive hypothesis, we obtain
p_{k-1}(s_{k})\geq\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{k}-)\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}\geq a_{k}\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}
, so

\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}}{p_{k-1}(s_{k})}\leq\frac{1}{a_{k}}.

Together with (
61
), this implies that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{t_{k}}p_{k}=\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}r_{k}p_{k-1}+p_{k-1}(t_{k})\cdot\frac{1-e^{a_{k}(s_{k}-t_{k})}}{a_{k}}

\displaystyle=p_{k-1}(t_{k})e^{a_{k}(s_{k}-t_{k})}\biggl(\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}}{p_{k-1}(s_{k})}-\frac{1}{a_{k}}\biggr)+\frac{p_{k-1}(t_{k})}{a_{k}}

\displaystyle\geq p_{k-1}(s_{k})\biggl(\frac{\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}}{p_{k-1}(s_{k})}-\frac{1}{a_{k}}\biggr)+\frac{p_{k-1}(t_{k})}{a_{k}}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{s_{k}}p_{k-1}+\frac{p_{k-1}(t_{k})-p_{k-1}(s_{k})}{a_{k}}=\int_{-\infty}^{t_{k}}p_{k-1}.

Combining this with (
59
) and (
60
) yields

\int_{s}^{t}p_{k}=\int_{-\infty}^{t_{k}}p_{k}-\int_{-\infty}^{s}r_{k}p_{k-1}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}p_{k-1}\geq\int_{-\infty}^{t_{k}}p_{k-1}-\int_{-\infty}^{s}p_{k-1}+\int_{t_{k}}^{t}p_{k-1}=\int_{s}^{t}p_{k-1}.

In view of (
60
) and part
(b)
of the inductive hypothesis, we conclude that if
(s_{k},t_{k})\subseteq(s,t)
and
s_{k}\leq s_{0}
, then

p_{0}(z)\int_{s}^{t}p_{k}=p_{0}(z)\int_{s}^{t}p_{k-1}\geq p_{k-1}(z)\int_{s}^{t}p_{0}\geq p_{k}(z)\int_{s}^{t}p_{0}

for
z\in\{s,t\}
, which proves
(b)
in this case. On the other hand, if
(s_{k},t_{k})\subseteq(s,t)
and
s_{k}>s_{0}
, then instead
a_{k}\in(-\infty,0]
, but the arguments are similar and hence omitted.

(c)
For
j\in[K]
such that
j>k
, we have
(s_{j},t_{j})\cap(s_{k},t_{k})=\emptyset
, so by (
60
) and part
(c)
of the inductive hypothesis,

\frac{p_{k}(z)}{p_{k}(s_{j})}=\frac{p_{k-1}(z)}{p_{k-1}(s_{j})}=\begin{cases}p_{0}(z)/p_{0}(s_{j})&\text{if }z\in[s_{j},t_{j}]\text{ for }j\in\mathbb{N}\text{ with }j>k\\
p_{j}(z)/p_{j}(s_{j})&\text{if }z\in[s_{j},t_{j}]\text{ for }j\in\mathbb{N}\text{ with }j<k.\end{cases}

(d)
There is nothing to prove when
s=t
, so let
s,t\in\mathcal{T}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
be such that
s<t
. If either
(s,t)\subseteq(-\infty,s_{k})
or
(s,t)\subseteq(t_{k},\infty)
, then by (
60
),
p_{k}=rp_{k-1}
on
[s,t]
for some
r\in\{1,r_{k},r_{k}^{-1}\}
. Thus, by part
(d)
of the inductive hypothesis,

p_{k}(t)-p_{k}(s)=r\bigl(p_{k-1}(t)-p_{k-1}(s)\bigl)\>\begin{cases}\leq r\psi_{0}^{*}(s)\int_{s}^{t}p_{k-1}=\psi_{0}^{*}(s)\int_{s}^{t}p_{k}\\[3.0pt]
\geq r\psi_{0}^{*}(t-)\int_{s}^{t}p_{k-1}=\psi_{0}^{*}(t-)\int_{s}^{t}p_{k},\end{cases}

as required. In the remaining case,
(s_{k},t_{k})\subseteq(s,t)
, and similarly by (
60
) and part
(d)
of the inductive hypothesis,

\displaystyle\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{k}-)\int_{s}^{s_{k}}p_{k}

\displaystyle\leq p_{k}(s_{k})-p_{k}(s)\leq\psi_{0}^{*}(s)\int_{s}^{s_{k}}p_{k},

\displaystyle\psi_{0}^{*}(t-)\int_{t_{k}}^{t}p_{k}

\displaystyle\leq p_{k}(t)-p_{k}(t_{k})\leq\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{k})\int_{t_{k}}^{t}p_{k}.

Moreover,
a_{k}\int_{s_{k}}^{t_{k}}p_{k}=a_{k}p_{k}(t_{k})\int_{s_{k}}^{t_{k}}e^{a(z-t_{k})}\,dz=p_{k}(t_{k})-p_{k}(s_{k})
and
a_{k}=\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{k})=\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{k}-)
, so

\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{k}-)\int_{s_{k}}^{t_{k}}p_{k}=p_{k}(t_{k})-p_{k}(s_{k})=\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{k})\int_{s_{k}}^{t_{k}}p_{k}.

Since
\psi_{0}^{*}
is decreasing,
combining this with the two inequalities above yields
(d)
.
∎

Lemma 22
.

For
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\lim_{k\to\infty}p_{k}(z)=\frac{p_{0}(s_{0})}{p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})}\cdot p_{0}^{*}(z).

(62)

Moreover, let
t_{\min}:=\inf\mathcal{T}
and
t_{\max}:=\sup\mathcal{T}
. If
s,t\in\mathcal{T}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
are such that
-\infty<s\vee t_{\min}\leq t\wedge t_{\max}<\infty
, then

\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{s}^{t}p_{k}=\frac{p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})}{p_{0}(s_{0})}\int_{s}^{t}p_{0}^{*}.

(63)

Proof.

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\phi_{k}:=\log p_{k}
and define
r_{k}
as in (
59
). Then as noted above,
\phi_{k}>-\infty
on
\mathcal{T}
, and Lemma
21
(c)
ensures that

\log r_{k}=\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{k})(t_{k}-s_{k})-\bigl(\phi_{k-1}(t_{k})-\phi_{k-1}(s_{k})\bigr)=\int_{s_{k}}^{t_{k}}\psi_{0}^{*}(z)\,dz-\bigl(\phi_{0}(t_{k})-\phi_{0}(s_{k})\bigr).

(64)

Fix
t\in\mathcal{T}
such that
t\geq s_{0}
, and for convenience, define
[K]=\emptyset
when
K=0
and
[K]=\mathbb{N}
when
K=\infty
. Let
\mathcal{K}
be the set of
\ell\in[K]
such that
(s_{\ell},t_{\ell})\subseteq(s_{0},t)
, so that
(s_{\ell},t_{\ell})\cap(s_{0},t)=\emptyset
for
\ell\in[K]\setminus\mathcal{K}
and hence
\mathcal{T}^{c}\cap(s_{0},t)=\bigcup_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}}(s_{\ell},t_{\ell})
. Similarly to the proof of Lemma
20
, let
U\sim U(0,1)
, so that
Z:=F_{0}^{-1}(U)\sim P_{0}
,
F_{0}(Z)=U
and
\psi_{0}^{*}(Z)=\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}(U)
. We have
\psi_{0}^{*}\leq 0
and
p_{0}>0
on
\mathcal{T}\cap(s_{0},t)
, and moreover
\hat{J}_{0}
is Lipschitz on
(F_{0}(s_{0}),F_{0}(t)]
with
\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}\leq 0
, so

\displaystyle\int_{s_{0}}^{t}\psi_{0}^{*}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{T}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{\psi_{0}^{*}(Z)}{p_{0}(Z)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Z\in\mathcal{T}\cap(s_{0},t)\}}\biggr)=\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}(U)}{\hat{J}_{0}(U)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{F_{0}^{-1}(U)\in\mathcal{T}\cap(s_{0},t)\}}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}(U)}{\hat{J}_{0}(U)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{F_{0}^{-1}(U)\in(s_{0},t]\}}\biggr)-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}}\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}(U)}{\hat{J}_{0}(U)}\mathbbm{1}_{\{F_{0}^{-1}(U)\in(s_{\ell},t_{\ell}]\}}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\int_{F_{0}(s_{0})}^{F_{0}(t)}\frac{\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}}{\hat{J}_{0}}-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}}\int_{F_{0}(s_{\ell})}^{F_{0}(t_{\ell})}\frac{\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}}{\hat{J}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\log\hat{J}_{0}\bigl(F_{0}(t)\bigr)-\log\hat{J}_{0}\bigl(F_{0}(s_{0})\bigr)-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}}\bigl\{\log\hat{J}_{0}\bigl(F_{0}(t_{\ell})\bigr)-\log\hat{J}_{0}\bigl(F_{0}(s_{\ell})\bigr)\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}(t)-\phi_{0}(s_{0})-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}}\bigl(\phi_{0}(t_{\ell})-\phi_{0}(s_{\ell})\bigr),

(65)

where the second and final equalities above follow from Lemma
18
and the fact that
s_{0},t,s_{\ell},t_{\ell}\in\mathcal{T}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
for
\ell\in[K]
. In addition, for all such
\ell
, we have
\phi_{\ell}(t)=\phi_{\ell-1}(t)+(\log r_{\ell})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\ell\in\mathcal{K}\}}
by the definition of
p_{\ell}
, and
\phi_{0}^{*}:=\log p_{0}^{*}
satisfies
\phi_{0}^{*}(t)-\phi_{0}^{*}(s_{0})=\int_{s_{0}}^{t}\psi_{0}^{*}
. Thus, by induction together with (
64
) and (
65
),

\displaystyle\phi_{k}(t)-\phi_{0}(s_{0})

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}(t)-\phi_{0}(s_{0})+\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}\cap[k]}\log r_{\ell}=\phi_{0}(t)-\phi_{0}(s_{0})-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}\cap[k]}\Bigl(\phi_{0}(t_{\ell})-\phi_{0}(s_{\ell})-\int_{s_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell}}\psi_{0}^{*}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\biggl(\int_{s_{0}}^{t}\psi_{0}^{*}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{T}}+\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}}\int_{s_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell}}\psi_{0}^{*}\biggr)+\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}:\ell>k}\Bigl(\phi_{0}(t_{\ell})-\phi_{0}(s_{\ell})-\int_{s_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell}}\psi_{0}^{*}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{s_{0}}^{t}\psi_{0}^{*}+\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}:\ell>k}\Bigl(\phi_{0}(t_{\ell})-\phi_{0}(s_{\ell})-\int_{s_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell}}\psi_{0}^{*}\Bigr)\to\phi_{0}^{*}(t)-\phi_{0}^{*}(s_{0})

as
k\to\infty
, which yields (
62
) for
z=t
. On the other hand, if
t\in\mathcal{T}
and
t<s_{0}
, then we can instead let
\mathcal{K}
be the set of
\ell\in[K]
such that
(s_{\ell},t_{\ell})\subseteq(t,s_{0})
, and deduce by similar reasoning that

\displaystyle\phi_{k}(t)-\phi_{0}(s_{0})

\displaystyle=\phi_{0}(t)-\phi_{0}(s_{0})+\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}\cap[k]}\Bigl(\phi_{0}(t_{\ell})-\phi_{0}(s_{\ell})-\int_{s_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell}}\psi_{0}^{*}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=-\int_{t}^{s_{0}}\psi_{0}^{*}-\sum_{\ell\in\mathcal{K}:\ell>k}\Bigl(\phi_{0}(t_{\ell})-\phi_{0}(s_{\ell})-\int_{s_{\ell}}^{t_{\ell}}\psi_{0}^{*}\Bigr)\to\int_{s_{0}}^{t}\psi_{0}^{*}=\phi_{0}^{*}(t)-\phi_{0}^{*}(s_{0})

as
k\to\infty
. Having proved that (
62
) holds for all
z\in\mathcal{T}
, we now consider
z\in\mathcal{T}^{c}
, for which there exists a unique
\ell\in[K]
such that
z\in(s_{\ell},t_{\ell})
. If
s_{\ell}>s_{0}
, then
p_{\ell}(z)=p_{\ell}(s_{\ell})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{\ell})(z-s_{\ell})}
, while if
s_{\ell}\leq s_{0}
, then
p_{\ell}(z)=p_{\ell}(t_{\ell})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{\ell})(z-t_{\ell})}
.
For
k>\ell
, it follows from Lemma
21
(c)
that

p_{k}(z)=\begin{cases}p_{k}(t_{\ell})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{\ell})(z-t_{\ell})}&\text{if }s_{\ell}\leq s_{0}\\
p_{k}(s_{\ell})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{\ell})(z-s_{\ell})}&\text{if }s_{\ell}>s_{0}.\end{cases}

Moreover,
s_{\ell},t_{\ell}\in\mathcal{T}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
, so if
s_{\ell}\leq s_{0}
, then

p_{k}(z)=p_{k}(t_{\ell})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{\ell})(z-t_{\ell})}\to\frac{p_{0}(s_{0})}{p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})}\cdot p_{0}^{*}(t_{\ell})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(s_{\ell})(z-t_{\ell})}=\frac{p_{0}(s_{0})}{p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})}\cdot p_{0}^{*}(z)

as
K\to\infty
, and (
62
) holds similarly when
s_{\ell}>s_{0}
.

Finally, let
s,t\in\mathcal{T}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
be such that
-\infty<s\vee t_{\min}\leq t\wedge t_{\max}<\infty
. If
t>t_{\max}
, then there exists a unique
j\in[K]
such that
t_{\max}=s_{j}<t_{j}=t=\infty
, and by Lemma
6
and its proof,
\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{\max})=\lim_{z\to\infty}\psi_{0}^{*}(z)\in(-\infty,0)
. Moreover, by Lemma
21
(c)
,

p_{k}(z)=p_{k}(t_{\max})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{\max})(z-t_{\max})}

for all
z>t_{\max}
and
k\geq j
. Similarly, if
s<t_{\min}
, then there exists a unique
\ell\in[K]
such that
-\infty=s=s_{\ell}<t_{\ell}=t_{\min}
, and
\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{\min}-)=\lim_{z\to-\infty}\psi_{0}^{*}(z)\in(0,\infty)
. In addition, by Lemma
21
(c)
,

p_{k}(z)=p_{k}(t_{\min})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{\min}-)(z-t_{\min})}

for all
z<t_{\min}
and
k\geq\ell
. Thus, for
k\geq j\vee\ell
, it follows from Lemma
21
(a)
that in all cases,

p_{k}(z)\leq\begin{cases}p_{0}(t_{\min})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{\min}-)(z-t_{\min})}\;\;&\quad\text{for }z\in(s,s\vee t_{\min})\\
\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}\;\;&\quad\text{for }z\in[s\vee t_{\min},t\wedge t_{\max}]\\
p_{0}(t_{\max})e^{\psi_{0}^{*}(t_{\max})(z-t_{\max})}\;\;&\quad\text{for }z\in(t\wedge t_{\max},t),\end{cases}

so the pointwise supremum
\sup_{k\geq j\vee\ell}p_{k}
is integrable on
(s,t)
, and hence (
63
) follows from (
62
) and the dominated convergence theorem.
∎

Proof of Proposition
10
.

(a)
We will prove the following stronger statement: for
s,t\in\mathcal{T}\cup\{-\infty,\infty\}
such that
s\leq t
, and
z\in\{s,t\}
, we have

\frac{p_{0}^{*}(z)}{F_{0}^{*}(t)-F_{0}^{*}(s)}\leq\frac{p_{0}(z)}{F_{0}(t)-F_{0}(s)}.

(66)

Assume that at least one of
s,t
is finite, since otherwise (
66
) holds trivially. Let
t_{\min}:=\inf\mathcal{T}
and
t_{\max}:=\sup\mathcal{T}
. If
-\infty<s\vee t_{\min}\leq t\wedge t_{\max}<\infty
, then by Lemma
22
,

p_{0}(z)\int_{s}^{t}p_{0}^{*}=\frac{p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})}{p_{0}(s_{0})}\cdot p_{0}(z)\lim_{k\to\infty}\int_{s}^{t}p_{k}\geq\lim_{k\to\infty}\frac{p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})}{p_{0}(s_{0})}\cdot p_{k}(z)\int_{s}^{t}p_{0}=p_{0}^{*}(z)\int_{s}^{t}p_{0}

(67)

for
z\in\{s,t\}
. Otherwise, it follows from (
67
) that

\displaystyle\displaystyle p_{0}(s)\int_{s}^{\infty}p_{0}^{*}=p_{0}(s)\cdot\sup_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\int_{s}^{t^{\prime}}p_{0}^{*}\geq p_{0}^{*}(s)\cdot\sup_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\int_{s}^{t^{\prime}}p_{0}=p_{0}^{*}(s)\int_{s}^{\infty}p_{0}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if }-\infty<s<t_{\max}=t=\infty

\displaystyle\displaystyle p_{0}(t)\int_{-\infty}^{t}p_{0}^{*}=p_{0}(t)\cdot\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}p_{0}^{*}\geq p_{0}^{*}(t)\cdot\sup_{s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}p_{0}=p_{0}^{*}(t)\int_{-\infty}^{t}p_{0}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if }-\infty=s=t_{\min}<t<\infty,

which completes the proof of (
66
). In particular, for
z\in\mathcal{T}
, taking
(s,t)=(-\infty,z)
and
(s,t)=(z,\infty)
in (
66
), we obtain

p_{0}(z)\int_{z}^{\infty}p_{0}^{*}\geq p_{0}^{*}(z)\int_{z}^{\infty}p_{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad p_{0}(z)\int_{-\infty}^{z}p_{0}^{*}\geq p_{0}^{*}(z)\int_{-\infty}^{z}p_{0},

which proves (
25
). Summing these inequalities yields
p_{0}(z)\geq p_{0}^{*}(z)
, as required.

(b)
Since
s_{0}\in\mathcal{T}
and
p_{0}^{*}(z)=p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})\exp\bigl(\int_{s_{0}}^{z}\psi_{0}^{*}\bigr)\leq p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})
for
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we deduce that
\|{p_{0}^{*}}\|_{\infty}=p_{0}^{*}(s_{0})\leq p_{0}(s_{0})\leq\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}
.
Finally, by Theorem
2
(c)
,
i^{*}(p_{0})=\bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}\big/V_{p_{0}}(\psi_{0}^{*})\bigr)^{1/2}=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\,d\psi_{0}^{*}
; see the equality case of (
49
). Similarly,
i(p_{0}^{*})=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}^{*}\,d\psi_{0}^{*}
, so because
p_{0}^{*}\leq p_{0}
on the support
\mathcal{T}
of the Lebesgue–Stieltjes measure induced by
\psi_{0}^{*}
, we have

i^{*}(p_{0})=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\,d\psi_{0}^{*}\geq-\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}^{*}\,d\psi_{0}^{*}=i(p_{0}^{*}),

as required.
∎

6.2
Additional examples for Section
2

Example 23
.

For densities
p_{0}
that are uniformly continuous and locally absolutely continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, it is possible to have
i^{*}(p_{0})<\infty=i(p_{0})
. This is unfavourable from the perspective of statistical efficiency because
\mathrm{ARE}^{*}(p_{0})=0
in this case. To construct an example of such a density
p_{0}
, let

J_{0}(u):=\begin{cases}u\biggl(2+\sin\Bigl(\dfrac{\pi}{4u}\Bigr)\biggr)\;&\text{for }u\in(0,1/2]\\
0&\text{for }u=0\end{cases}\qquad\text{and}\qquad J_{0}(u):=J_{0}(1-u)\;\;\text{for }u\in(1/2,1],

so that
J_{0}
is continuous on
[0,1]
and infinitely differentiable on
(0,1)
, with
u\leq J_{0}(u)\leq 3u
for all
u\in[0,1/2]
. The least concave majorant
\hat{J}_{0}
of
J_{0}
is given by
\hat{J}_{0}(u)=3\min(u,1-u)
for
u\in[0,1]
. In addition, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\biggl(\int_{0}^{1}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\geq\int_{0}^{1}|J_{0}^{\prime}|=2\int_{0}^{1/2}\,\Bigl|2+\sin\Bigl(\frac{u}{4\pi}\Bigr)-\frac{\pi}{4u}\cos\Bigl(\frac{u}{4\pi}\Bigr)\Bigr|=\infty,

so
J_{0}
is not of bounded variation on
(0,1)
. Next, defining
Q_{0}\colon(0,1)\to\mathbb{R}
by
Q_{0}(u):=\int_{1/2}^{u}1/J_{0}
, we have
\lim_{u\nearrow 1}Q_{0}(u)=-\lim_{u\searrow 0}Q_{0}(u)=\int_{0}^{1/2}1/J_{0}\geq\int_{0}^{1/2}1/(3u)\,du=\infty
. Then by Lemma
17
,
Q_{0}
is a strictly increasing, continuously differentiable bijection from
(0,1)
to
\mathbb{R}
, and
p_{0}:=(Q_{0}^{-1})^{\prime}
is a strictly positive, uniformly continuous density on
\mathbb{R}
with density quantile function
J_{0}
. Moreover, by Lemma
1
,
p_{0}^{\prime}=(J_{0}^{\prime}\circ F_{0})\,p_{0}
is continuous, so
p_{0}
is locally absolutely continuous on
\mathbb{R}
. By Remark
3
,

i^{*}(p_{0})=\int_{0}^{1}\bigl(\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}\bigr)^{2}=\int_{0}^{1}3^{2}=9<\infty=\int_{0}^{1}(J_{0}^{\prime})^{2}=i(p_{0}),

so indeed
\mathrm{ARE}^{*}(p_{0})=0
. By modifying this construction slightly, one can also exhibit a unimodal density
p_{0}
with
\mathrm{ARE}^{*}(p_{0})=0
.

Example 24
.

For
z\in\mathbb{R}
, let

p_{0}(z):=\frac{1}{2}(1+z^{2})^{-3/2},

so that
p_{0}
is the density of
W/\sqrt{2}
when
W\sim t_{2}
. Then by
Dümbgen et al., 2011
, the log-concave maximum likelihood projection
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
is a standard Laplace density given by

p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}(z)=\frac{1}{2}e^{-|z|}

for
z\in\mathbb{R}
, with score function
\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}(\cdot)=-\sgn(\cdot)
. Therefore, the corresponding regression
M
-estimator
\hat{\beta}_{\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}}\in\argmax_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta)=\argmin_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta|
is the LAD estimator. On the other hand, by straightforward computations,

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=(\log p_{0})^{\prime}(z)=-\frac{3z}{1+z^{2}},

\displaystyle\psi_{0}^{*}(z)

\displaystyle=(\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}\circ F_{0})(z)=\psi_{0}\bigl((z\wedge 3^{-1/2})\vee(-3^{-1/2})\bigr)=\Bigl(-\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{4}\Bigr)\vee\psi_{0}(z)\wedge\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{4}

for
z\in\mathbb{R}
, so similarly to Example
12
, the optimal convex loss is a Huber-like function given by

\ell_{0}^{*}(z)=-\log p_{0}^{*}(z)=-\int_{0}^{z}\psi_{0}^{*}+\log 2=\begin{cases}\,\dfrac{3\log\bigl((1+z^{2})/2\bigr)}{2}&\text{for }z\in[-3^{-1/2},3^{-1/2}]\\[6.0pt]
\,\dfrac{3\bigl(\sqrt{3}|z|-1+\log(4/9)\bigr)}{4}&\text{for }z\in\mathbb{R}\setminus[-3^{-1/2},3^{-1/2}].\end{cases}

This means that
p_{0}^{*}\neq p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
, and
\hat{\beta}_{\psi_{0}^{*}}\in\argmin_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\ell_{0}^{*}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta)
is usually different from
\hat{\beta}_{\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}}
, with the ratio of their asymptotic covariances being equal to

\frac{V_{p_{0}}(\psi_{0}^{*})}{V_{p_{0}}(\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}})}=\frac{1/i^{*}(p_{0})}{1/\bigl(4p_{0}(0)^{2}\bigr)}=\frac{1}{i^{*}(p_{0})}=-\biggl(\int_{-1/\sqrt{3}}^{1/\sqrt{3}}\;p_{0}\,d\psi_{0}\biggr)^{-1}=\frac{80}{93}\approx 0.860.

Example 25
.

For
\alpha,\sigma>0
, consider the symmetrised Pareto density
p_{0}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
given by

p_{0}(z):=\frac{\alpha\sigma^{\alpha}}{2(|z|+\sigma)^{\alpha+1}}.

The corresponding distribution has a finite mean if and only if
\alpha>1
, in which case its log-concave maximum likelihood projection
(
Dümbgen et al., 2011
)
is a Laplace density given by

p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}(z):=\frac{\alpha-1}{2\sigma}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(\alpha-1)|z|}{\sigma}\biggr)

(68)

for
z\in\mathbb{R}
; see
Chen and Samworth, 2013
or
Samworth and Shah, 2025
. On the other hand, for general
\alpha,\sigma>0
,
routine calculations yield

J_{0}(u)=\frac{\alpha\bigl(2\min(u,1-u)\bigr)^{1+1/\alpha}}{2\sigma}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{J}_{0}(u)=\frac{\alpha\min(u,1-u)}{\sigma}

for
u\in[0,1]
. Since
p_{0}
is symmetric about 0, it follows that
\psi_{0}^{*}(z)=(1-2\mathbbm{1}_{\{z\geq 0\}})\alpha/\sigma
and hence

\ell_{0}^{*}(z)=-\log p_{0}^{*}(z)=\log\Bigl(\frac{\alpha}{2\sigma}\Bigr)-\frac{\alpha|z|}{\sigma},\qquad p_{0}^{*}(z)=\frac{\alpha}{2\sigma}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\alpha|z|}{\sigma}\Bigr)

for
z\in\mathbb{R}
. Therefore,
p_{0}^{*}
is also a Laplace density, but while
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}\neq p_{0}^{*}
when
\alpha>1
, both corresponding regression
M
-estimators
\hat{\beta}_{\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}}
and
\hat{\beta}_{\psi_{0}^{*}}
in (
23
) minimise
\beta\mapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}|Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta|
over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and hence coincide when there is a unique minimiser (i.e. least absolute deviation estimator).

Example 26
.

For
\rho\in(0,1)
and
\mu>0
, let
p_{0}
be the two-component Laplace mixture density given by

p_{0}(z)=\frac{1-\rho}{2}e^{-|z+\mu|}+\frac{\rho}{2}e^{-|z-\mu|}

for
z\in\mathbb{R}
. The corresponding score function
\psi_{0}=(\log p_{0})^{\prime}=p_{0}^{\prime}/p_{0}
satisfies

\psi_{0}(z)=\begin{cases}1\;\;&\text{for }z<-\mu\\
\dfrac{\rho e^{z}-(1-\rho)e^{-z}}{\rho e^{z}+(1-\rho)e^{-z}}\;\;&\text{for }z\in(-\mu,\mu)\\
-1\;\;&\text{for }z>\mu,\end{cases}

so
\psi_{0}
is strictly increasing on
(-\mu,\mu)
and constant on both
(-\infty,\mu)
and
(\mu,\infty)
. Since
J_{0}^{\prime}=\psi_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}
on
(0,1)\setminus\{F_{0}(-\mu),F_{0}(\mu)\}
, it follows that
J_{0}
is convex on
[F_{0}(-\mu),F_{0}(\mu)]
while being linear on both
[0,F_{0}(-\mu)]
and
[F_{0}(\mu),1]
. Therefore,
\hat{J}_{0}=J_{0}
on
[0,F_{0}(-\mu)]\cup[F_{0}(\mu),1]
and
\hat{J}_{0}
is linear on
[F_{0}(-\mu),F_{0}(\mu)]
with
J_{0}\bigl(F_{0}(\pm\mu)\bigr)=p_{0}(\pm\mu)
, so

\psi_{0}^{*}(z)=\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}\bigl(F_{0}(z)\bigr)=\begin{cases}1\;\;&\text{for }z<-\mu\\
\dfrac{p_{0}(\mu)-p_{0}(-\mu)}{F_{0}(\mu)-F_{0}(-\mu)}=2\rho-1\;\;&\text{for }z\in[-\mu,\mu)\\
-1\;\;&\text{for }z\geq\mu,\end{cases}

and hence

p_{0}^{*}(z)=\begin{cases}Ce^{z+\mu}\;\;&\text{for }z\leq-\mu\\
Ce^{(2\rho-1)(z+\mu)}\;\;&\text{for }z\in[-\mu,\mu]\\
Ce^{(2\rho-1)2\mu}\cdot e^{-(z-\mu)}\;\;&\text{for }z\geq\mu,\end{cases}\qquad\text{where }C:=\frac{1}{1+e^{(2\rho-1)2\mu}+\int_{0}^{2\mu}e^{(2\rho-1)z}\,dz}.

By direct calculation,
p_{0}^{*}(-\mu)=C<p_{0}(-\mu)
and
p_{0}^{*}(\mu)=Ce^{(2\rho-1)2\mu}<p_{0}(\mu)
, so
\|{p_{0}^{*}}\|_{\infty}=p_{0}^{*}(-\mu)\vee p_{0}^{*}(\mu)<p_{0}(-\mu)\vee p_{0}(\mu)=\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}
and

i^{*}(p_{0})=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\,d\psi_{0}^{*}=2(1-\rho)\cdot p_{0}(-\mu)+2(1+\rho)\cdot p_{0}(\mu)>2(1-\rho)\cdot p_{0}^{*}(-\mu)+2(1+\rho)\cdot p_{0}^{*}(\mu)=i(p_{0}^{*}).

Proposition 27
.

For
\rho\in(0,1)
,
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma>0
, let
P_{0}:=(1-\rho)N(\mu_{1},\sigma^{2})+\rho N(\mu_{2},\sigma^{2})
and denote by
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
and
p_{0}^{*}
its log-concave maximum likelihood and Fisher divergence projections respectively.

(a)

If
|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}|\leq 2\sigma
, then
P_{0}
has density
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}=p_{0}^{*}
.

(b)

If
|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}|>2\sigma
, then
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}\neq p_{0}^{*}
. Moreover,
\psi_{0}^{*}
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, and there exist
z_{1}\in(-\infty,a]
and
z_{2}\in[b,\infty)
such that
\psi_{0}^{*}
is decreasing on
(-\infty,z_{1}]\cup[z_{2},\infty)
and constant on
[z_{1},z_{2}]
.

Proof.

By
Cule et al., 2010
and
Samworth and Shah, 2025
,
p_{0}
is log-concave if and only if
|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}|\leq 2\sigma
. We will slightly refine this result by first noting that

\displaystyle p_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\rho}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(z-\mu_{1})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\biggr)+\frac{\rho}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(z-\mu_{2})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\psi_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=(\log p_{0})^{\prime}(z)=\frac{1}{p_{0}(z)}\biggl\{\frac{(1-\rho)(\mu_{1}-z)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(z-\mu_{1})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\biggr)+\frac{\rho(\mu_{2}-z)}{\sqrt{2\pi}\sigma^{3}}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(z-\mu_{2})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\psi_{0}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=(\log p_{0})^{\prime\prime}(z)=-\frac{g(z)}{2\pi\sigma^{4}p_{0}(z)^{2}}\exp\biggl(-\frac{(z-\mu_{1})^{2}+(z-\mu_{2})^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\biggr)

for
z\in\mathbb{R}
, where

\displaystyle g(z):=(1-\rho)^{2}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{\mu_{2}-\mu_{1}}{2\sigma^{2}}(2z-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\rho^{2}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{2}-\mu_{1}}{2\sigma^{2}}(2z-\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\Bigr)

\displaystyle+\rho(1-\rho)\Bigl\{2-\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}}{\sigma}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr\}

is a convex function of
z
with

\min_{z\in\mathbb{R}}g(z)=\rho(1-\rho)\Bigl\{4-\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}}{\sigma}\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr\}.

(a)
If
|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}|\leq 2\sigma
, then
g\geq 0
on
\mathbb{R}
and hence
P_{0}
has a log-concave density
p_{0}=p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}=p_{0}^{*}
.

(b)
Otherwise, if
|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}|>2\sigma
, then there exist
a<b
such that
g\geq 0
and hence
\psi_{0}^{\prime}\leq 0
on
(-\infty,a]\cup[b,\infty)
, while
g\leq 0
and
\psi_{0}^{\prime}\geq 0
on
[a,b]
. Therefore, by
Dümbgen et al., 2011
, there exist
a^{\prime}\in(-\infty,a]
and
b^{\prime}\in[b,\infty)
such that
\phi:=\log p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
agrees with
\phi_{0}:=\log p_{0}
on
(-\infty,a^{\prime}]\cup[b^{\prime},\infty)
, and
\phi
is affine on
[a^{\prime},b^{\prime}]
.

Suppose for a contradiction that
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
is differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
. Then
\psi_{0}(a^{\prime})=\phi_{0}^{\prime}(a^{\prime})=\phi^{\prime}(a^{\prime})=\phi^{\prime}(b^{\prime})=\psi_{0}(b^{\prime})
, so defining
\ell\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\ell(z):=\phi_{0}(a^{\prime})+\psi_{0}(a^{\prime})(z-a^{\prime})
, we have
\phi=\ell
on
[a^{\prime},b^{\prime}]
and
\ell(z)=\phi_{0}(b^{\prime})+\psi_{0}(b^{\prime})(z-b^{\prime})
for
z\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
\phi_{0}
is concave on both
(-\infty,a]
and
[b,\infty)
while being convex on
[a,b]
, it follows that
\phi_{0}\leq\ell
on
(-\infty,a]\cup[b,\infty)
and

\phi_{0}(z)\leq\frac{b-z}{b-a}\,\phi_{0}(a)+\frac{z-a}{b-a}\,\phi_{0}(b)\leq\frac{b-z}{b-a}\,\ell(a)+\frac{z-a}{b-a}\,\ell(b)=\ell(z)=\phi(z)

for all
z\in[a,b]
. Therefore,
\log p_{0}=\phi_{0}\leq\phi=\log p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
on
\mathbb{R}
, but since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}=1
, we must have
p_{0}=p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
because both functions are continuous. However, this contradicts the fact that
p_{0}
is not log-concave, so
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}
is not differentiable (at either
a^{\prime}
or
b^{\prime}
).

On the other hand,
p_{0}
and its corresponding quantile function
F_{0}^{-1}
are differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
, so
J_{0}=p_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}
is differentiable on
(0,1)
. By Lemma
53
,
\hat{J}_{0}
is continuously differentiable on
(0,1)
, so
(\log p_{0}^{*})^{(\mathrm{R})}=\psi_{0}^{*}=\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}\circ F_{0}
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}
. Therefore,
\log p_{0}^{*}
is differentiable on
\mathbb{R}
and hence
p_{0}^{\mathrm{ML}}\neq p_{0}^{*}
.

Moreover, by the first line of the proof of
(b)
,
J_{0}^{\prime}=\psi_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1}
is increasing on
[F_{0}(a),F_{0}(b)]
, and decreasing on both
(0,F_{0}(a)]
and
[F_{0}(b),1)
. We deduce from Lemma
54
that there exist
z_{1}\in(-\infty,a]
and
z_{2}\in[b,\infty)
such that
\psi_{0}^{*}
is decreasing on
(-\infty,z_{1}]\cup[z_{2},\infty)
and constant on
[z_{1},z_{2}]
.
∎

6.3
Background and proofs for Section
3

Section
6.3.1
uses population-level results about the antitonic score projection from Section
2
to motivate the alternating algorithms in Section
3
; this material is intended as background and is not required elsewhere. In the subsequent three subsections, we prove the antitonic efficiency results in Sections
3.3
and
3.4
. Section
6.3.2
establishes key consistency results for our kernel-based score estimators, which are then used to prove Theorems
13
and
14
in Sections
6.3.3
and
6.3.4
respectively. Some techniques from the classical
M
-estimation literature are adapted to provide a common template for both semiparametric models, whose efficient scores and information matrices are derived in Section
6.3.5
. Despite the similarities among Lemmas
32
to
37
and their proofs, there are nevertheless some subtle differences.

6.3.1
Joint optimisation for the score and regression coefficients

For
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, define
q_{\beta}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by

q_{\beta}(z):=\mathbb{E}p_{0}\bigl(z-X_{1}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta)\bigr),

so that
q_{\beta}
is the density of
Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta=\varepsilon_{1}+X_{1}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta)
. Our approach to estimating
\beta_{0}
with data-driven convex loss functions is motivated by the following population-level joint optimisation problem. We seek to minimise the augmented score matching objective

\displaystyle Q(\beta,\psi):=D_{q_{\beta}}(\psi)=\mathbb{E}\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi^{2}\bigl(z+X_{1}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta)\bigr)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz+2\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\bigl(z-X_{1}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta)\bigr)\,d\psi(z)\biggr)

(69)

jointly over
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta})
satisfying

\mathbb{E}\bigl(X_{1}\psi(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta)\bigr)=0.

(70)

Recall from (
14
) that if
\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta})
is locally absolutely continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, then

Q(\beta,\psi)=\mathbb{E}\psi^{2}(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta)+2\mathbb{E}\psi^{\prime}(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta).

The constraint (
70
) is the population analogue of the estimating equations (
2
) based on
\psi
. It forces
\beta
to be a minimiser of the convex population risk function
b\mapsto\mathbb{E}\ell(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}b)=:L_{\psi}(b)
over
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
,
7
7

7

Indeed,
\ell
is convex with
\psi=-\ell^{(\mathrm{R})}
, so for
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\ell(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta^{\prime})\geq\ell(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta)+(\beta-\beta^{\prime})^{\top}X_{1}\psi(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta).

If
\beta
satisfies (
70
), then taking expectations in the display above shows that
L_{\psi}(\beta^{\prime})\geq L_{\psi}(\beta)
for all
\beta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
.
where
\ell
is any negative antiderivative of
\psi
, and where
\mathcal{D}_{\psi}
is the convex set of
b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
L_{\psi}(b)
is well-defined in
[-\infty,\infty]
.

The following proposition characterises the global joint minimiser of our constrained score matching optimisation problem. For
j\in[d]
, write
\mathsf{e}_{j}
for the
j
th standard basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Proposition 28
.

For an absolutely continuous density
p_{0}
with
\mathcal{S}(p_{0})=\mathbb{R}
, let
\psi^{*}_{0}
be the projected score function defined in (
17
). Assume that
i^{*}(p_{0})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}<\infty
and that
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is positive definite. Let

\Gamma:=\bigl\{(\beta,\psi):\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\,\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta}),\,\mathbb{E}\bigl(X_{1}\psi(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta)\bigr)=0\bigr\}.

(a)

Suppose that
p_{0}
is symmetric. Denote by
\Psi_{\downarrow}^{\mathrm{anti}}(q_{\beta})
the set of all
\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta})
such that
\psi(-z)=-\lim_{z^{\prime}\nearrow z}\psi(z^{\prime})
, and let
\Gamma^{\mathrm{anti}}:=\{(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma:\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}^{\mathrm{anti}}(q_{\beta})\}
. Then

\displaystyle(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*})=\argmin_{(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{anti}}}Q(\beta,\psi).

(b)

If instead
X_{1d}=1
almost surely, then

\displaystyle\bigl(\beta_{0}+c\mathsf{e}_{d},\psi_{0}^{*}(\cdot+c)\bigr)\in\argmin_{(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma}\,Q(\beta,\psi)

for all
c\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, all minimisers are of this form.

Thus, when we restrict attention to right-continuous versions of antisymmetric functions
\psi
in the setting of Proposition
28
(a)
, the unique minimiser of our population-level optimisation problem is given by the pair consisting of the vector
\beta_{0}
of true regression coefficients, and the antitonic score projection
\psi_{0}^{*}
of the error density
p_{0}
. This motivates our statistical methodology below, where we seek to minimise sample versions of this population-level objective. The proof below shows that for any
(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{anti}}
, we also have
(\beta_{0},\psi)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{anti}}
; moreover,
Q(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*})=\inf_{\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}^{\mathrm{anti}}(q_{\beta_{0}})}Q(\beta_{0},\psi)=-1/i^{*}(p_{0})<0
. On the other hand, if
\beta\neq\beta_{0}
, and if
\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}^{\mathrm{anti}}(q_{\beta})
is such that
(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{anti}}
, then
Q(\beta,\psi)\geq 0
. In other words, feasible pairs
(\beta,\psi)
with
\beta\neq\beta_{0}
are well-separated from the optimal solution in terms of their objective function values.

In Proposition
28
(b)
, where the errors need not have mean zero and where we no longer restrict attention to antisymmetric
\psi
, the pair
(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*})
still minimises our objective up to appropriate translations to account for the lack of identifiability of the intercept term. Indeed, the joint distribution of
(X_{1},Y_{1})
does not change if we replace
\beta_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{1}
with
\beta_{0}+c\mathsf{e}_{d}
and
\varepsilon_{1}-c
respectively, for any
c\in\mathbb{R}
. This explains why the minimiser in
(b)
is not unique; observe that
\psi_{0}^{*}(\cdot+c)
is the projected score corresponding to the density
p_{0}(\cdot+c)
of
\varepsilon_{1}-c
. Nevertheless,
(b)
indicates that translations of the intercept term are the
only
source of non-uniqueness, so that the first
d-1
components of
\beta_{0}
can still be recovered by solving the constrained optimisation problem above on the population level. This provides some insight into the reason that (
42
) in Section
3.4
holds without any centring assumption on the errors. The constraint
\mathbb{E}\zeta(\varepsilon_{1})=0
restores the identifiability of the intercept
\mu_{0}
.

Proof.

In both
(a)
and
(b)
,
X_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{1}
are independent, so by the final assertion of Lemma
1
, we have
\mathbb{E}\bigl\{X_{1}\psi_{0}^{*}(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta_{0})\bigr\}=\mathbb{E}(X_{1})\,\mathbb{E}\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{1})=0
. Therefore,
(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*})
satisfies the constraint (
70
). Moreover,
Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta_{0}=\varepsilon_{1}
has density
q_{\beta_{0}}=p_{0}
, so by Theorem
2
(
b
,
c
),

Q(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*})=D_{p_{0}}(\psi_{0}^{*})=-i^{*}(p_{0})=\inf_{\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(p_{0})}D_{p_{0}}(\psi)=\inf_{\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(p_{0})}Q(\beta_{0},\psi).

(a)
Since
p_{0}
is symmetric and has a continuous distribution function
F_{0}
, we have
F_{0}(z)=1-F_{0}(-z)
for every
z\in\mathbb{R}
, so
J_{0}(u):=(p_{0}\circ F_{0}^{-1})(u)=p_{0}\bigl(-F_{0}^{-1}(1-u)\bigr)=J_{0}(1-u)
and hence
\hat{J}_{0}(u)=\hat{J}_{0}(1-u)
for all
u\in[0,1]
. For every
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
F_{0}(z)\in(0,1)
because
\mathcal{S}(p_{0})=\mathbb{R}
, so

\psi_{0}^{*}(-z)=\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}\bigl(F_{0}(-z)\bigr)=\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}\bigl(1-F_{0}(z)\bigr)=-\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{L})}\bigl(F_{0}(z)\bigr)=-\lim_{u\nearrow F_{0}(z)}\hat{J}_{0}^{(\mathrm{R})}(u)=-\lim_{z^{\prime}\nearrow z}\psi_{0}^{*}(z^{\prime}),

(71)

where the penultimate equality follows from
Rockafellar, 1997
. Thus,
\psi_{0}^{*}\in\Psi_{\downarrow}^{\mathrm{anti}}(p_{0})=\Psi_{\downarrow}^{\mathrm{anti}}(q_{\beta_{0}})
.

Next, we claim that if
(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma^{\mathrm{anti}}
is such that
\beta\neq\beta_{0}
, then necessarily
Q(\beta,\psi)\geq 0>Q(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*})
, which proves
(a)
. Indeed,
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})
is positive definite by assumption, so
T:=X_{1}^{\top}(\beta-\beta_{0})
satisfies
\mathbb{E}(T^{2})=(\beta-\beta_{0})^{\top}\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})(\beta-\beta_{0})>0
and hence
\mathbb{P}(T\neq 0)>0
. By (
70
),
\mathbb{E}\bigl(T\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-T)\bigr)=0
, so because
T
and
\varepsilon_{1}
are independent, there exists
t\neq 0
such that
\mathbb{E}|\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-t)|<\infty
. Since
\varepsilon_{1}\overset{d}{=}-\varepsilon_{1}
and
\psi
agrees Lebesgue almost everywhere on
\mathbb{R}
with an antisymmetric function, we have
\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}+t)=\mathbb{E}\psi(-\varepsilon_{1}+t)=-\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-t)\in\mathbb{R}
. Finally, since
|\psi(\varepsilon_{1})|\leq|\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-t)|\vee|\psi(\varepsilon_{1}+t)|
, this shows that
\mathbb{E}|\psi(\varepsilon_{1})|<\infty
. Thus,
\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1})=\mathbb{E}\psi(-\varepsilon_{1})=-\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1})
, so again because
T
and
\varepsilon_{1}
are independent,

\mathbb{E}\bigl(T\psi(\varepsilon_{1})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}(T)\,\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1})=0=\mathbb{E}\bigl(T\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-T)\bigr).

(72)

For
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

T^{2}\int_{0}^{1}p_{0}(z+sT)\,ds=T\int_{z}^{z+T}p_{0}(w)\,dw=\int_{\mathbb{R}}Tp_{0}(w)\bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{\{w-T<z\leq w\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{w<z\leq w-T\}}\bigr)\,dw.

Hence, by Fubini’s theorem and the independence of
T
and
\varepsilon_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}T^{2}p_{0}(z+sT)\,d\psi(z)\,ds

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(w)\int_{\mathbb{R}}T\bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{\{w-T<z\leq w\}}-\mathbbm{1}_{\{w<z\leq w-T\}}\bigr)\,d\psi(z)\,dw

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}T\bigl(\psi(w)-\psi(w-T)\bigr)\,p_{0}(w)\,dw

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl(T\psi(\varepsilon_{1})\bigr)-\mathbb{E}\bigl(T\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-T)\bigr)=0.

Defining
g\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
by
g(s):=-\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}T^{2}p_{0}(z+sT)\,d\psi(z)\geq 0
, we therefore have
\int_{0}^{1}g=0
, so
g=0
Lebesgue almost everywhere. Now
p_{0}
is continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, so by Fatou’s lemma,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq-\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}T^{2}p_{0}(z+T)\,d\psi(z)=g(1)\leq\liminf_{s\nearrow 1}g(s)=0,

(73)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq-\mathbb{E}(T^{2})\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(z)\,d\psi(z)=-\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}T^{2}p_{0}(z)\,d\psi(z)=g(0)\leq\liminf_{s\searrow 0}g(s)=0.

(74)

By (
73
),
\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(z+T)\mathbbm{1}_{\{T\neq 0\}}\,d\psi(z)=0
almost surely. Moreover, since
\mathbb{E}(T^{2})>0
, (
74
) implies that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(z+T)\mathbbm{1}_{\{T=0\}}\,d\psi(z)=\mathbbm{1}_{\{T=0\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(z)\,d\psi(z)=0
almost surely, so

\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\bigl(z+X_{1}^{\top}(\beta-\beta_{0})\bigr)\,d\psi(z)=\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(z+T)\,d\psi(z)=0.

(75)

Hence, by the definition of
Q(\beta,\psi)
in (
69
),

Q(\beta,\psi)=\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi^{2}\bigl(z-X_{1}^{\top}(\beta-\beta_{0})\bigr)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz\geq 0>Q(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*}),

as claimed.

(b)
For
c\in\mathbb{R}
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta})
and
x=(\tilde{x},1)\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\times\mathbb{R}
, we have
x^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta)=x^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta-c\mathsf{e}_{d})+c
, so
\psi_{c}(\cdot):=\psi(\cdot+c)
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{c}^{2}\bigl(z+x^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta-c\mathsf{e}_{d})\bigr)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi^{2}\bigl(z+x^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta)\bigr)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz,

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\bigl(z-x^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta-c\mathsf{e}_{d})\bigr)\,d\psi_{c}(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\bigl(z-x^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta-c\mathsf{e}_{d})-c\bigr)\,d\psi(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\bigl(z-x^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta)\bigr)\,d\psi(z).

It follows that if
X_{1d}=1
almost surely, then
Q(\beta+c\mathsf{e}_{d},\psi_{c})=Q(\beta,\psi)
.

For
\beta_{c}:=\beta_{0}+c\mathsf{e}_{d}
, we have
q_{\beta_{c}}(\cdot)=p_{0}(\cdot+c)
, and
\psi_{c}\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta_{c}})
if and only if
\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta_{0}})=\Psi_{\downarrow}(p_{0})
. Thus,

0>-i^{*}(p_{0})=Q(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*})=\inf_{\psi\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(p_{0})}Q(\beta_{0},\psi)=\inf_{\psi_{c}\in\Psi_{\downarrow}(q_{\beta_{c}})}Q(\beta_{c},\psi_{c})

(76)

for all
c\in\mathbb{R}
.

Next, for any
(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma
, we claim that there exists
c\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-c)=0
. Indeed,
X_{1d}=1
almost surely, so
T=X_{1}^{\top}(\beta-\beta_{0})
satisfies
\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-T)=\mathbb{E}\psi(Y_{1}-X_{1}^{\top}\beta)=0
. By the independence of
T
and
\varepsilon
, it follows that
\varPsi(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi(z-t)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz=\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-t)
is well-defined and finite for
P_{T}
-almost every
t\in\mathbb{R}
, where
P_{T}
denotes the distribution of
T
. Since
\psi
is decreasing,
\varPsi
is increasing, so there exist
t_{1}\leq t_{2}
in the support of
P_{T}
such that
-\infty<\varPsi(t_{1})\leq 0\leq\varPsi(t_{2})<\infty
, and
|\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-t)|\leq|\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-t_{1})|\vee|\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-t_{2})|
for all
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
. It follows from the dominated convergence theorem that
\varPsi(t)
is continuous in
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
, so by the intermediate value theorem, there exists
c\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
such that
\mathbb{E}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-c)=\varPsi(c)=0
, as claimed.

By (
70
) together with the independence of
\tilde{T}:=T-c=X_{1}^{\top}(\beta-\beta_{c})
and
\varepsilon_{1}
, we deduce that

\mathbb{E}\bigl(\tilde{T}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-c)\bigr)=0=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\tilde{T}\psi(\varepsilon_{1}-c-\tilde{T})\bigr).

This is similar to (
72
) in the proof of
(a)
, but with
T
and
\varepsilon_{1}
replaced by
\tilde{T}
and
\varepsilon_{1}-c
respectively.

Suppose now that
\beta\neq\beta_{c^{\prime}}
for any
c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
. Since
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})
is positive definite by assumption,
\mathbb{E}(\tilde{T}^{2})=(\beta-\beta_{c})^{\top}\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})(\beta-\beta_{c})>0
. By arguing similarly to
(a)
and noting that
p_{c}(\cdot):=p_{0}(\cdot+c)
is the density of
\varepsilon_{1}-c
, we conclude as in (
75
) that

\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\bigl(z+X_{1}^{\top}(\beta-\beta_{0})\bigr)\,d\psi(z)=\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(z+T)\,d\psi(z)=\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{c}(z+\tilde{T})\,d\psi(z)=0.

Thus, if
(\beta,\psi)\in\Gamma
is such that
\beta\neq\beta_{c^{\prime}}
for any
c^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
, then

Q(\beta,\psi)=\mathbb{E}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi^{2}\bigl(z-X_{1}^{\top}(\beta-\beta_{0})\bigr)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz\geq 0>Q(\beta_{0},\psi_{0}^{*}).

Together with (
76
), this completes the proof.
∎

6.3.2
Consistency of kernel-based score estimators

Lemma
29
below shows that the initial score estimates
\tilde{\psi}_{n,j}
are consistent in
L^{2}(P_{0})
. It extends the proof of
van der Vaart, 1998
to kernel density estimators
\tilde{p}_{n,j}
based on out-of-sample residuals
\{\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}:i\in I_{j+1}\}
instead of the unobserved regression errors
\varepsilon_{i}
. The three folds of the data will be denoted by
\mathcal{D}_{j}\equiv\mathcal{D}_{n,j}:=\{(X_{i},Y_{i}):i\in I_{j}\}
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Lemma 29
.

Suppose that for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, we have
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}+\mu+X_{i}^{\top}b_{n}
for all
i\in I_{j+1}
, where
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
is fixed and
b_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
as
n\to\infty
. If in addition
(A1)
,
(A3)
and
(A4)
hold, then for each
j
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\tilde{\psi}_{n,j}(z)-\psi_{0}(z-\mu)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z-\mu)\,dz\overset{p}{\to}0

as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the result for
\tilde{\psi}_{n}\equiv\tilde{\psi}_{n,1}
and
\mu=0
, since
\tilde{\psi}_{n,2},\tilde{\psi}_{n,3}
are obtained by permuting the three folds of the data (which have roughly the same size), and the density and score function of
\varepsilon_{i}^{\prime}:=\varepsilon_{i}+\mu
are
p_{0}(\cdot-\mu)
and
\psi_{0}(\cdot-\mu)
respectively. By
(A1)
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,
\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{0}^{\prime}|\leq\bigl(\int_{\{p_{0}>0\}}(p_{0}^{\prime})^{2}/p_{0}\bigr)^{1/2}=i(p_{0})^{1/2}<\infty
. Since
K
is twice continuously differentiable and supported on
[-1,1]
by
(A3)
, we have
\int_{\mathbb{R}}K^{2}\vee\int_{\mathbb{R}}(K^{\prime})^{2}<\infty
. Denote by
P_{X}
the distribution of
X_{1}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
y\in\mathbb{R}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
h\equiv h_{n}
, let
\tilde{p}_{n}(y)\equiv\tilde{p}_{n,1}(y)=|I_{2}|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{2}}K_{h}(y-\hat{\varepsilon}_{i})
and

\displaystyle p_{n}(y):=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}(y)\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{h}(y-z-x^{\top}b_{n})p_{0}(z)\,dz\,dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n})K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x).

We have
nh_{n}^{2}=(nh_{n}^{3}\gamma_{n}^{2})(h_{n}\gamma_{n}^{2})^{-1}\to\infty
by
(A4)
, so
\|{b_{n}}\|=o_{p}(h)
as
n\to\infty
. Moreover, since
p_{0}
is uniformly continuous on
\mathbb{R}
, it follows from the bounded convergence theorem that
p_{n}(y)\overset{p}{\to}p_{0}(y)
for every
y\in\mathbb{R}
. By Fubini’s theorem,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{n}-p_{0}|

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl\{p_{0}(y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n})-p_{0}(y)\bigr\}K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)\biggr|\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{0}^{\prime}(z)|(\mathbbm{1}_{\{y<z\leq y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n}<z\leq y\}})\,dz\,K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)\,dy

\displaystyle=\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{0}^{\prime}(z)|\,dz\biggr)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|uh+x^{\top}b_{n}|K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{0}^{\prime}|\biggr)\biggl(h\int_{\mathbb{R}}|u|K(u)\,du+\|{b_{n}}\|\cdot\mathbb{E}\|{X_{1}}\|\biggr)=O_{p}(h).

(77)

By
(A4)
and the dominated convergence theorem, we may differentiate under the integral sign to obtain

\displaystyle p_{n}^{\prime}(y)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}(y)\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{h}^{\prime}(y-z-x^{\top}b_{n})p_{0}(z)\,dz\,dP_{X}(x).

For
y\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\Var\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}(y)\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|I_{2}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{h}(y-z-x^{\top}b_{n})^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz\,dP_{X}(x)\leq\frac{\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K^{2}}{|I_{2}|h},

(78)

\displaystyle\Var\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}(y)\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|I_{2}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K_{h}^{\prime}(y-z-x^{\top}b_{n})^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz\,dP_{X}(x)\leq\frac{\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(K^{\prime})^{2}}{|I_{2}|h^{3}}.

(79)

Since
|I_{2}|h_{n}^{3}\to\infty
by
(A4)
, it follows from Lemma
47
that
\tilde{p}_{n}(y)-p_{n}(y)\overset{p}{\to}0
and
\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}(y)-p_{n}^{\prime}(y)\overset{p}{\to}0
. Defining
\tau(z):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{0}^{\prime}(y-z)-p_{0}^{\prime}(y)|\,dy\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{0}^{\prime}|
for
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\lim_{z\to 0}\tau(z)=0
by continuity of translation
(
Folland, 1999
, Proposition 8.5)
. Thus, since
b_{n}\overset{p}{\to}0
, it follows from the bounded convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{n}^{\prime}-p_{0}^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl\{p_{0}^{\prime}(y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n})-p_{0}^{\prime}(y)\bigr\}K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)\biggr|\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\tau(uh+x^{\top}b_{n})K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)\overset{p}{\to}0.

This means that every subsequence of
(p_{n})
has a further subsequence
(p_{n_{k}})
such that with probability 1, we have
p_{n_{k}}^{\prime}\to p_{0}^{\prime}
and hence
(p_{n_{k}}^{\prime})^{2}/p_{n_{k}}\to(p_{0}^{\prime})^{2}/p_{0}
Lebesgue almost everywhere. By the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\frac{p_{n}^{\prime}(y)^{2}}{p_{n}(y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}^{\prime}(y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n})K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)\bigr)^{2}}{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}(y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n})K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(p_{0}^{\prime})^{2}}{p_{0}}(y-uh-x^{\top}b_{n})K(u)\,du\,dP_{X}(x)

for
y\in\mathbb{R}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, so by Fubini’s theorem,

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(p_{n}^{\prime})^{2}}{p_{n}}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(p_{0}^{\prime})^{2}}{p_{0}}

(80)

for all
n
. Thus, by applying a slight generalisation of Scheffé’s lemma
(
van der Vaart, 1998
, Lemma 2.29)
to subsequences of
(p_{n})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigl(\frac{p_{n}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{n}}}-\frac{p_{0}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{0}}}\Bigr)^{2}\overset{p}{\to}0

(81)

as
n\to\infty
. Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{0}^{2}\,p_{0}=i(p_{0})<\infty
and
\alpha_{n}\to\infty
while
\gamma_{n}\to 0
, we can argue similarly to the proof of
van der Vaart, 1998
and deduce by the dominated convergence theorem that

\mathbb{E}\biggl(\int_{\tilde{S}_{n,1}^{c}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0})^{2}\,p_{0}\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{1}\biggr)\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{0}(y)^{2}\,p_{0}(y)\cdot\bigl\{\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}(y)|>\alpha_{n}\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)+\mathbb{P}\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}(y)<\gamma_{n}\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)\bigr\}\,dy\overset{p}{\to}0.

Moreover, on
\tilde{S}_{n,1}
, we have

\displaystyle|\tilde{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}|\sqrt{p_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}|}{\tilde{p}_{n}}|\sqrt{p_{0}}-\sqrt{p_{n}}|+\frac{\sqrt{p_{n}}}{\tilde{p}_{n}}|\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}-p_{n}^{\prime}|+\frac{|p_{n}^{\prime}|}{\sqrt{p_{n}}}\frac{|p_{n}-\tilde{p}_{n}|}{|\tilde{p}_{n}|}+\Bigl|\frac{p_{n}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{n}}}-\frac{p_{0}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{0}}}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\gamma_{n}}|p_{0}-p_{n}|^{1/2}+\frac{\sqrt{p_{n}}}{\gamma_{n}}|\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}-p_{n}^{\prime}|+\frac{|p_{n}^{\prime}|}{\sqrt{p_{n}}}\frac{|p_{n}-\tilde{p}_{n}|}{\gamma_{n}}+\Bigl|\frac{p_{n}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{n}}}-\frac{p_{0}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{0}}}\Bigr|,

so by (
78
)–(
81
) and
(A4)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\,\mathbb{E}\biggl(\int_{\tilde{S}_{n,1}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0})^{2}\,p_{0}\biggm|\mathcal{D}_{1}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\alpha_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{n}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{n}-p_{0}|+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{p_{n}(y)}{\gamma_{n}^{2}}\Var\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}^{\prime}(y)\,|\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)\,dy+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(p_{n}^{\prime})^{2}}{\gamma_{n}^{2}\,p_{n}}(y)\Var\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}(y)\,|\,\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)\,dy

\displaystyle\hskip 312.9803pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigl(\frac{p_{n}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{n}}}-\frac{p_{0}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{0}}}\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\alpha_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{n}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|p_{n}-p_{0}|+\frac{\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(K^{\prime})^{2}}{|I_{2}|h_{n}^{3}\gamma_{n}^{2}}+\frac{\|{p_{0}}\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}K^{2}}{|I_{2}|h_{n}\gamma_{n}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{(p_{0}^{\prime})^{2}}{p_{0}}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigl(\frac{p_{n}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{n}}}-\frac{p_{0}^{\prime}}{\sqrt{p_{0}}}\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=O_{p}\Bigl(\frac{\alpha_{n}^{2}}{\gamma_{n}^{2}}h_{n}\Bigr)+O_{p}\Bigl(\frac{1}{|I_{2}|h_{n}^{3}\gamma_{n}^{2}}\Bigr)+o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1).

Therefore, by Lemma
47
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0})^{2}\,p_{0}\overset{p}{\to}0
.
∎

Next, we use Lemma
29
and properties of antitonic projections (Section
6.5
) to show that the projected score estimates
\hat{\psi}_{n,j}
are consistent in
L^{2}(P_{0})
for the population-level antitonic projection
\psi_{0}^{*}
of
\psi_{0}
.

Lemma 30
.

In the setting of Lemma
29
, suppose that
(A1)
–
(A5)
hold. Then for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,j}(z)-\psi_{0}^{*}(z-\mu)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z-\mu)\,dz\overset{p}{\to}0.

(82)

Moreover,
\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}(z-t)\,p_{0}(z-\mu)\,dz\in\mathbb{R}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, and for any sequence
(v_{n})
satisfying
v_{n}(\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n})\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s,t\in[-v_{n},v_{n}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n,j}(z-t)-\hat{\psi}_{n,j}(z-s)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z-\mu)\,dz

\displaystyle\overset{p}{\to}0,

(83)

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}(v_{n}):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,t\in[-v_{n},v_{n}]:\\
s\neq t\end{subarray}}\,\biggl|\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}(t)-\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}(s)}{t-s}-i^{*}(p_{0})\biggr|

\displaystyle\overset{p}{\to}0

(84)

as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

Similarly to the previous lemma, it suffices to prove this result when
\mu=0
and
j=1
, so we will drop the index
j
in all of our notation below. In the terminology of Section
6.5
,
\hat{\psi}_{n}\equiv\hat{\psi}_{n,1}\in\Pi_{\downarrow}(\tilde{\psi}_{n},\tilde{P}_{n})
is defined as an
L^{2}(\tilde{P}_{n})
-antitonic projection of the initial score estimator
\tilde{\psi}_{n}\equiv\tilde{\psi}_{n,1}
, where
\tilde{P}_{n}\equiv\tilde{P}_{n,1}
denotes the distribution with density
\tilde{p}_{n}\equiv\tilde{p}_{n.1}
. For each
n
, let

\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}:=\widehat{\mathcal{M}}_{\mathrm{R}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}\circ F_{0}^{-1})\circ F_{0}\in\Pi_{\downarrow}(\tilde{\psi}_{n},P_{0})

denote a corresponding
L^{2}(P_{0})
-antitonic projection of
\tilde{\psi}_{n}
. Then by (
143
)
and the definition (
31
) of
\tilde{\psi}_{n}
, we have
\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}}\|_{\infty}\vee\|{\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}}\|_{\infty}\leq\|{\tilde{\psi}_{n}}\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n}<\infty
. We have
\tilde{\psi}_{n}\in L^{2}(P_{0})\cap L^{2}(\tilde{P}_{n})
for all
n
, so by (
141
) in Lemma
50
together with the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}-\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\tilde{\psi}_{n})\,d(\tilde{P}_{n}-P_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\|{(\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\psi}_{n})(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\tilde{\psi}_{n})}\|_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{p}_{n}-p_{0}|\leq 4\Bigl(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\gamma_{n}}\Bigr)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(|\tilde{p}_{n}-p_{n}|+|p_{n}-p_{0}|),

(85)

where
p_{n}(y)=\mathbb{E}\bigl(\tilde{p}_{n}(y)\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr)
for
y\in\mathbb{R}
. By
(A1)
,
\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{1}|^{\delta})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}|z|^{\delta}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz<\infty
for some
\delta\in(0,1]
. Recall from
(A4)
that
\rho\in\bigl(0,\delta/(\delta+1)\bigr)
. By Lemma
45
,
(A5)
,
(A3)
and the fact that
b_{n}\overset{p}{\to}0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{p}_{n}-p_{n}|\Bigm|\mathcal{D}_{1}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2^{1-2\rho}\|{K}\|_{\infty}C_{\delta,\rho}^{\rho}}{(|I_{2}|h)^{\rho}}(1+h)^{\delta(1-\rho)}\Bigl(1+\int_{\mathbb{R}}|z|^{\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}p_{0}(z-x^{\top}b_{n})\,dP_{X}(x)\,dz\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{2^{1-2\rho}\|{K}\|_{\infty}C_{\delta,\rho}^{\rho}}{(|I_{2}|h)^{\rho}}(1+h)^{\delta(1-\rho)}\bigl(1+\mathbb{E}\bigl\{|\varepsilon_{1}+X_{1}^{\top}b_{n}|^{\delta}\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}_{1}\bigr\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2^{1-2\rho}\|{K}\|_{\infty}C_{\delta,\rho}^{\rho}}{(|I_{2}|h)^{\rho}}(1+h)^{\delta(1-\rho)}\bigl\{1+\mathbb{E}(|\varepsilon_{1}|^{\delta})+\mathbb{E}(\|{X_{1}}\|^{\delta})\cdot\|{b_{n}}\|^{\delta}\bigr\}=O_{p}\bigl((nh)^{-\rho}\bigr)

as
n\to\infty
and
h\to 0
with
nh\to\infty
, where
C_{\delta,\rho}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|z|)^{-\delta(1-\rho)/\rho}\,dz<\infty
. Thus, by Lemma
47
and
(A4)
,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}|\tilde{p}_{n}-p_{n}|=O_{p}\bigl((nh)^{-\rho}\bigr)=O_{p}(n^{-2\rho/3})
. Combining this with (
77
), (
85
) and
(A4)
yields
\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}-\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}^{2}=O_{p}\bigl((h_{n}\vee n^{-2\rho/3})(\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n})^{2}\bigr)=o_{p}(1)
as
n\to\infty
.
Hence, by applying the triangle inequality for
\|{{\cdot}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}
together with (
142
) and Lemma
29
, we obtain

\displaystyle\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}\biggr)^{1/2}=\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}-\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}+\|{\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}-\psi_{0}^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}-\tilde{\psi}_{n}^{*}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}+\|{\tilde{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{0})}\overset{p}{\to}0,

which proves (
82
).

For
t\in\mathbb{R}
, let
p_{t}(\cdot):=p_{0}(\cdot+t)
. We have
\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}}\|_{\infty}\leq\|{\tilde{\psi}_{n}}\|_{\infty}\leq\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n}
for each
n
, so
\hat{\Psi}_{n}(t)\equiv\hat{\Psi}_{n,1}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\psi}_{n}\,p_{t}
is finite for every
t
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 3\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t}}-\sqrt{p_{0}}\bigr)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}\sqrt{p_{0}}-\psi_{0}^{*}\sqrt{p_{0}}\bigr)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{0}}-\sqrt{p_{t}}\bigr)^{2}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq 3\biggl(\Bigl(\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\gamma_{n}}\Bigr)^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t}}-\sqrt{p_{0}}\bigr)^{2}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{0}}-\sqrt{p_{t}}\bigr)^{2}\biggr)

(86)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-t)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(z)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 3\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl\{\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-t)-\psi_{0}^{*}(z-t)\bigr)^{2}+\bigl(\psi_{0}^{*}(z-t)-\psi_{0}^{*}(z)\bigr)^{2}+\bigl(\psi_{0}^{*}(z)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(z)\bigr)^{2}\bigr\}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\qquad=3\biggl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{t}+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\psi_{0}^{*}(z-t)-\psi_{0}^{*}(z)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz+\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}\biggr).

(87)

Since
i(p_{0})<\infty
by
(A1)
, Lemma
42
implies that

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t}}-\sqrt{p_{0}}\bigr)^{2}=O(t^{2})

(88)

as
t\to 0
. Moreover, by
(A2)
,
\psi_{0}^{*}
satisfies the hypotheses of Lemma
44
, so

\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{0}}-\sqrt{p_{t}}\bigr)^{2}\to 0\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\psi_{0}^{*}(z-t)-\psi_{0}^{*}(z)\bigr)^{2}p_{0}(z)\,dz\to 0

(89)

as
t\to 0
. Therefore, letting
(v_{n})
be such that
v_{n}(\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n})\to 0
, we deduce from (
82
), (
86
) and (
89
) that

\sup_{t\in[-v_{n},v_{n}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{t}\overset{p}{\to}0.

(90)

Hence by (
87
) and (
89
),

\sup_{s,t\in[-v_{n},v_{n}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-t)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-s)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz\leq 4\sup_{t\in[-v_{n},v_{n}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-t)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(z)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz\overset{p}{\to}0.

This yields (
83
). Next, by Lemma
44
,
\Psi_{0}^{*}(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{0}^{*}(z-t)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{0}^{*}\,p_{t}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
t\in[-t_{0},t_{0}]
, and by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\displaystyle\bigl|\hat{\Psi}_{n}(t)-\hat{\Psi}_{n}(s)-\bigl(\Psi_{0}^{*}(t)-\Psi_{0}^{*}(s)\bigr)\bigr|^{2}

\displaystyle=\biggl|\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})(p_{t}-p_{s})\biggr|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t}}+\sqrt{p_{s}}\bigr)^{2}\biggr\}\biggl\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t}}-\sqrt{p_{s}}\bigr)^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\biggl\{2\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}(p_{t}+p_{s})\biggr\}\biggl\{\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t}}-\sqrt{p_{s}}\bigr)^{2}\biggr\}

for
s,t\in[-t_{0},t_{0}]
. By (
88
),

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t}}-\sqrt{p_{s}}\bigr)^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\sqrt{p_{t-s}}-\sqrt{p_{0}}\bigr)^{2}=O\bigl((t-s)^{2}\bigr)
as
s,t\to 0
, so it follows from (
90
) that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,t\in[-v_{n},v_{n}]:\\
s\neq t\end{subarray}}\,\biggl|\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{n}(t)-\hat{\Psi}_{n}(s)-\bigl(\Psi_{0}^{*}(t)-\Psi_{0}^{*}(s)\bigr)}{t-s}\biggr|=o_{p}(1)

(91)

as
n\to\infty
. By Lemma
44
and Theorem
2
(c)
,
(\Psi_{0}^{*})^{\prime}(0)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{0}\,d\psi_{0}^{*}=\bigl(\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}\big/V_{p_{0}}(\psi_{0}^{*})\bigr)^{1/2}=i^{*}(p_{0})\in(0,\infty)
, so

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}s,t\in[-v_{n},v_{n}]:\\
s\neq t\end{subarray}}\,\biggl|\frac{\Psi_{0}^{*}(t)-\Psi_{0}^{*}(s)}{t-s}-i^{*}(p_{0})\biggr|\to 0

as
n\to\infty
. Combining this with (
91
) yields (
84
).
∎

6.3.3
Proof of Theorem
13

Corollary 31
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, define
\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
by
\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}(t):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z-t)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz
. Then under the hypotheses of Theorem
13
, the conclusions of Lemma
30
hold with
\mu=0
for
(\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}})
and
(\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}})
in place of
(\hat{\psi}_{n,j})
and
(\hat{\Psi}_{n,j})
respectively.

Proof.

It suffices to consider
j=1
, so we drop the index
j
in all of our notation below. The pilot residuals in Step 3 can be written as
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}=Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\bar{\beta}_{n}^{(1)}=\varepsilon_{i}+X_{i}^{\top}b_{n}
for
i\in I_{2}
, where
b_{n}:=\beta_{0}-\bar{\beta}_{n}^{(1)}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
by assumption. Therefore, the hypotheses of Lemmas
29
and
30
are satisfied with
\mu=0
. Since
p_{0}
is symmetric,
\psi_{0}(z)=p_{0}^{\prime}(z)/p_{0}(z)=-\psi_{0}(-z)
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
. By (
71
),
\psi_{0}^{*}(z)=-\psi_{0}^{*}(-z)
for Lebesgue almost every
z\in\mathbb{R}
, so

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl(\frac{\hat{\psi}_{n}(z)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(-z)}{2}-\frac{\psi_{0}^{*}(z)-\psi_{0}^{*}(-z)}{2}\biggr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(z)-\psi_{0}^{*}(z)\bigr)^{2}+\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(-z)-\psi_{0}^{*}(-z)\bigr)^{2}}{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}

for each
n
. Similarly, for
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z-t)

\displaystyle-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z-s)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-t)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-s)\bigr)^{2}+\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}(z+t)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(z+s)\bigr)^{2}}{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

and

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-t)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(t-z)}{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\hat{\psi}_{n}(z-t)-\hat{\psi}_{n}(t+z)}{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{\Psi}_{n}(t)-\hat{\Psi}_{n}(-t)}{2}.

The desired conclusions for
(\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}})
and
(\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}})
therefore follow from Lemma
30
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
13
relies on the following ‘asymptotic equicontinuity’ result,
which is similar to
Bickel, 1975
.

Lemma 32
.

For
b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, define

R_{n,j}(b):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\Bigl\{\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\Bigl(\varepsilon_{i}-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)-\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\Bigl(\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}.

Fix
M>0
and let
B_{M}:=\{b\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|{b}\|\leq M\}
. Under the hypotheses of Theorem
13
, it holds for every
j\in\{1,2,3\}
that

\sup_{b\in B_{M}}\|{R_{n,j}(b)-R_{n,j}(0)}\|=o_{p}(1)

as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider
j=1
, so we drop the
j
subscript from
\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}
,
\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}
and
R_{n,j}
; we also write
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\equiv\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{D}_{1}\cup\mathcal{D}_{2}\cup\{X_{i}:i\in I_{3}\}
. We initially fix
b\in B_{M}
. For
i\in I_{3}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}bn^{-1/2})\,|\,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr\}=\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(X_{i}^{\top}bn^{-1/2})
, so
\mathbb{E}\bigl(R_{n}(b)\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)=0
. By
(A5)
and Cauchy–Schwarz,
\max_{i\in[n]}|X_{i}^{\top}b|\,\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n}=o_{p}(n^{1/2})
, so it follows from the weak law of large numbers and Corollary
31
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl(\|{R_{n}(b)-R_{n}(0)}\|^{2}\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\tr\Cov\bigl(R_{n}(b)-R_{n}(0)\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\tr\Cov\bigl(X_{i}\{\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}bn^{-1/2})-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i})\}\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\|{X_{i}}\|^{2}\Var\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}bn^{-1/2})-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i})\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\|{X_{i}}\|^{2}\Bigr)\max_{i\in I_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z-X_{i}^{\top}bn^{-1/2})-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z)\bigr)^{2}\,p_{0}(z)\,dz

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)\,o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1)

(92)

as
n\to\infty
, where the second equality holds because the summands
X_{i}\{\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}bn^{-1/2})-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i})\}
are conditionally independent given
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. Thus, by Lemma
47
,
\|{R_{n}(b)-R_{n}(0)}\|\overset{p}{\to}0
for every
b\in B_{M}
.

Fix
\epsilon\in(0,1)
. There exists a
\epsilon M
-Euclidean covering set
B_{M,\epsilon}\subseteq B_{M}
such that
|B_{M,\epsilon}|\leq 3\epsilon^{-d}

(e.g.
Wainwright, 2019
, Example 5.8)
, so since
d
is fixed as
n\to\infty
, it follows by a union bound that

\max_{b\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\|{R_{n}(b)-R_{n}(0)}\|\overset{p}{\to}0.

For every
b\in B_{M}
, there exists
\pi_{b}\in B_{M,\epsilon}
such that
\|{b-\pi_{b}}\|\leq\epsilon M
. Since
\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}
is decreasing and
\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}
is increasing for each
n
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\varepsilon_{i}-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}\pi_{b}-\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\varepsilon_{i}-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)\leq\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\varepsilon_{i}-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}\pi_{b}+\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr),

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\frac{X_{i}^{\top}\pi_{b}-\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\Bigl(\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)\leq\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\frac{X_{i}^{\top}\pi_{b}+\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)

for
b^{\prime}\in\{b,\pi_{b}\}
. Now for
b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}
, let

r_{n}(b^{\prime}):=\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggl\{\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\varepsilon_{i}-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}+\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\varepsilon_{i}-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}-\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)\biggr\}.

Then

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in B_{M}}\|R_{n}(b)-R_{n}(\pi_{b})\|\leq\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\bigl\{r_{n}(b^{\prime})+\mathbb{E}\bigl(r_{n}(b^{\prime})\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)\bigr\}.

By
(A5)
,
V_{n}:=\max_{i\in I_{3}}M(1+\epsilon)n^{-1/2}\|{X_{i}}\|=o_{p}(\gamma_{n}/\alpha_{n})
, so we deduce from Corollary
31
, Lemma
48
and the independence of
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2},\mathcal{D}_{3}
that

\displaystyle\Var\bigl(r_{n}(b^{\prime})\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{k\in I_{3}}\frac{\|{X_{k}}\|^{2}}{n}\max_{i\in I_{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\biggl\{\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(z-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}+\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(z-\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}-\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)\biggr\}^{2}p_{0}(z)\,dz\overset{p}{\to}0.

Together with a union bound over
b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}
, this shows that
\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\bigl\{r_{n}(b^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(r_{n}(b^{\prime})\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)\bigr\}=o_{p}(1)
. Moreover, recalling the definition of
\Delta_{n}
in (
84
), we have
\Delta_{n}(V_{n})=o_{p}(1)
by
(A5)
and Corollary
31
, so

\displaystyle\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}\bigl(r_{n}(b^{\prime})\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggl\{\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}+\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)-\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\frac{X_{i}^{\top}b^{\prime}-\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{2\epsilon M\|{X_{i}}\|^{2}}{n}\bigl(i^{*}(p_{0})+\Delta_{n}(V_{n})\bigr)=\frac{2\epsilon Mi^{*}(p_{0})}{3}\,\mathbb{E}(\|{X_{1}}\|^{2})+o_{p}(1).

Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{b\in B_{M}}\|{R_{n}(b)-R_{n}(0)}\|\leq\sup_{b\in B_{M}}\|R_{n}(b)-R_{n}(\pi_{b})\|+\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\|{R_{n}(b^{\prime})-R_{n}(0)}\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\bigl\{r_{n}(b^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}\bigl(r_{n}(b^{\prime})\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)\bigr\}+2\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}\bigl(r_{n}(b^{\prime})\bigm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr)+\max_{b^{\prime}\in B_{M,\epsilon}}\|{R_{n}(b^{\prime})-R_{n}(0)}\|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{4\epsilon Mi^{*}(p_{0})}{3}\,\mathbb{E}(\|{X_{1}}\|^{2})+o_{p}(1)

as
n\to\infty
. Since this holds for all
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, the desired conclusion follows.
∎

As a first step towards proving the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\dagger}
and
\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\ddagger}
in Theorem
13
, we show in Lemma
33
below that they are
\sqrt{n}
-consistent estimators of
\beta_{0}
. To this end, we exploit the convexity of the induced loss functions
z\mapsto\hat{\ell}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{sym}}(z)=-\int_{0}^{z}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}
, similarly to
He and Shao, 2000
. We denote the Euclidean unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by
S^{d-1}:=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\|u\|=1\}
.

Lemma 33
.

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
j\in\{1,2,3\}
and
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\bigl\{\|{\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0}}\|>t\bigr\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\biggl\{\inf_{u\in S^{d-1}}u^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i}-tX_{i}^{\top}u)\leq 0\biggr\},

(93)

\displaystyle\bigl\{\|{\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\ddagger}-\beta_{0}}\|>t\bigr\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\biggl\{\inf_{u\in S^{d-1}}u^{\top}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i}-tX_{i}^{\top}u)\leq 0\biggr\}.

(94)

Consequently, under the hypotheses of Theorem
13
, the following statements hold as
n\to\infty
:

(a)

\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})=O_{p}(1)
and
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\ddagger}-\beta_{0})=O_{p}(1)
;

(b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\bigl(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}\bigr)=o_{p}(1)
and
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\bigl(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\ddagger}\bigr)=o_{p}(1)
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider
j=1
. We write
\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\equiv\hat{\psi}_{n,1}^{\mathrm{anti}}
and
\hat{\ell}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}\equiv\hat{\ell}_{n,1}^{\mathrm{sym}}
. The function
\beta\mapsto\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\hat{\ell}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta)=:\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\beta)
is convex on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with
\hat{\beta}_{n}\equiv\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(1)}\in\argmin_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\beta)
, so
h\mapsto\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}\bigl((1-h)\hat{\beta}_{n}+h\beta_{0}\bigr)=:G_{n}(h)
is convex and increasing on
[0,\infty)
. Since

g_{n}(h):=-(\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\bigl(\varepsilon_{i}-(1-h)X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})\bigr)

is a subgradient of
G_{n}
at
h
, it follows that
g_{n}(h)\geq 0
for every
h>0
. Hence, if
\|{\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}}\|>t>0
, then

\displaystyle 0\leq g\biggl(1-\frac{t}{\|{\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}}\|}\biggr)

\displaystyle=-(\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\biggl(\varepsilon_{i}-tX_{i}^{\top}\frac{\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}}{\|{\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}}\|}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq-\|{\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}}\|\inf_{u\in S^{d-1}}u^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i}-tX_{i}^{\top}u),

(95)

so (
93
) holds. By analogous reasoning based on
\beta\mapsto\hat{L}_{n}^{\ddagger}(\beta):=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}\hat{\ell}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{sym}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta)
, we obtain (
94
).

(a)
Fix
M>0
and write
\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\equiv\hat{\Psi}_{n,1}^{\mathrm{anti}}
. By
(A5)
,
V_{n}:=\max_{i\in I_{3}}Mn^{-1/2}\|{X_{i}}\|=o_{p}(\gamma_{n}/\alpha_{n})
. We have
\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(0)=0
and
|I_{3}|/n\to 1/3
, so it follows from Corollary
31
, (
84
) and Lemma
48
that

\displaystyle\sup_{u\in S^{d-1}}\Biggl\|\,\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{X_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\Bigl(\frac{MX_{i}^{\top}u}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)-\frac{Mi^{*}(p_{0})}{3}\,\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})u\,\Biggr\|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{u\in S^{d-1}}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{\|{X_{i}}\|}{\sqrt{n}}\biggl|\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\Bigl(\frac{MX_{i}^{\top}u}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)-\frac{Mi^{*}(p_{0})X_{i}^{\top}u}{\sqrt{n}}\biggr|+Mi^{*}(p_{0})\,\biggl\|\,\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}}{n}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})}{3}\biggr\|_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{M\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\|{X_{i}}\|^{2}}{n}\cdot\Delta_{n}(V_{n})+Mi^{*}(p_{0})\biggl\|\,\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}}{n}-\frac{\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})}{3}\biggr\|_{\mathrm{op}}=o_{p}(1).

(96)

Since
\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})
is positive definite by
(A5)
, its minimum eigenvalue
\lambda_{\min}
is strictly positive. Then by (
96
) and Lemma
32
,

\displaystyle\inf_{u\in S^{d-1}}u^{\top}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{X_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\Bigl(\varepsilon_{i}-\frac{MX_{i}^{\top}u}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\inf_{u\in S^{d-1}}u^{\top}\biggl\{R_{n}(Mu)+\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\frac{X_{i}}{\sqrt{n}}\hat{\Psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}\Bigl(\frac{MX_{i}^{\top}u}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{u\in S^{d-1}}u^{\top}\Bigl(R_{n}(0)+\frac{Mi^{*}(p_{0})}{3}\,\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})u\Bigr)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\geq-\|{R_{n}(0)}\|+\frac{M\lambda_{\min}i^{*}(p_{0})}{3}+o_{p}(1).

(97)

Writing
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{D}_{1}\cup\mathcal{D}_{2}\cup\{X_{i}:i\in I_{3}\}
, we have

\mathbb{E}\biggl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i})-\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{i})\bigr)\biggm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\biggr)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}-\psi_{0}^{*})\,p_{0}=0

because
p_{0}
is symmetric and
\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}},\psi_{0}^{*}
are antisymmetric. Together with Corollary
31
,
this implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl(\biggl\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i})-\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{i})\bigr)\biggr\|^{2}\biggm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\|{X_{i}}\|^{2}(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}(\varepsilon_{i})\!\bigm|\!\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\|{X_{i}}\|^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}\overset{p}{\to}0.

We have
\mathbb{E}\bigl(X_{i}\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{i})\bigr)=\mathbb{E}(X_{i})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{0}^{*}\,p_{0}=0
for each
i
, so by the central limit theorem,

R_{n}(0)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(\varepsilon_{i})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{i})+o_{p}(1)=O_{p}(1).

(98)

Since
M\lambda_{\min}i^{*}(p_{0})>0
, we deduce from (
93
) and (
97
) that
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\|{\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0}}\|\geq M/\sqrt{n})\to 0
as
M\to\infty
, so
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{0})=O_{p}(1)
as
n\to\infty
. The
\sqrt{n}
-consistency of
\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\ddagger}
follows similarly from (
94
).

(b)
Since the errors
\varepsilon_{i}
have an absolutely continuous density
p_{0}
, the conditional distribution of
(Y_{i}:i\in I_{3})
given
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}_{1}\cup\mathcal{D}_{2}\cup\{X_{i}:i\in I_{3}\}
is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{I_{3}}
. We used
\mathcal{D}_{1}\cup\mathcal{D}_{2}
to obtain the convex function
\hat{\ell}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}
, whose subdifferential at
z\in\mathbb{R}
is

\partial\hat{\ell}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(z)=\bigl[\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z),\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z-)\bigr],

where
\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z-):=\lim_{z^{\prime}\nearrow z}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z^{\prime})
. Since
\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}
is decreasing,
A:=\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}:\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z)<\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(z-)\bigr\}
is countable. Thus, applying Lemma
46
to the linear subspace
W:=\bigl\{(X_{i}^{\top}\beta)_{i\in I_{3}}:\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\bigr\}
of dimension at most
d
, we have

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\max_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta\in A\}}\leq d\biggm|\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\biggr)=1.

(99)

Moreover,
\hat{\beta}_{n}\in\argmin_{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\beta)
, so writing
\partial\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\beta)
for the subdifferential of the convex function
\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}
at
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

(
Rockafellar, 1997
, p. 315)
, we have

0\in\partial\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(\hat{\beta}_{n})=\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\partial\hat{\ell}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n}),

so there exist
a_{i}\in\partial\hat{L}_{n}^{\mathrm{sym}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n})
for
i\in I_{3}
such that
\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}a_{i}=0
. For each
i\in I_{3}
, we have
\bigl|a_{i}-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n})\bigr|\leq 2\|{\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}}\|_{\infty}\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n}\in A\}}\leq 2(\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n})\,\mathbbm{1}_{\{Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n}\in A\}}
, so by (
99
),

\Biggl|\,\sum_{i\in I_{3}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n})\Biggr|\leq\sum_{i\in I_{3}}\|{X_{i}}\|\cdot\bigl|a_{i}-\hat{\psi}_{n}^{\mathrm{anti}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n})\bigr|\leq 2d\max_{i\in I_{3}}\|{X_{i}}\|\,\frac{\alpha_{n}}{\gamma_{n}}

almost surely. Assumption
(A5)
ensures that the right-hand side is
o_{p}(n^{1/2})
as
n\to\infty
, so
(b)
holds for
\hat{\beta}_{n}=\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(1)}
, and the result for
\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\ddagger}
follows similarly by considering
\hat{L}_{n}^{\ddagger}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
13
.

For
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, Lemma
33
(b)
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\bigl(X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})\bigr)+R_{n,j}\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\bigl(\varepsilon_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})\bigr)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)})=o_{p}(1).

(100)

and Lemma
33
(a)
yields

\biggl\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})\biggr\|\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}\|{X_{i}}\|^{2}\|{\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})}\|=O_{p}(1).

Moreover, by
(A5)
and Lemma
33
(a)
,
\max_{i\in I_{j+2}}|X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})|\,\alpha_{n}/\gamma_{n}=o_{p}(1)
as
n\to\infty
, so arguing similarly to (
96
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}^{\mathrm{anti}}\bigl(X_{i}^{\top}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})\bigr)=\frac{i^{*}(p_{0})}{3}\,\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})+o_{p}(1).

(101)

From (
101
) and (
100
), followed by Lemma
32
and Lemma
33
(a)
, and then (
98
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{i^{*}(p_{0})}{3}\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle=-R_{n,j}\bigl(\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})\bigr)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=-R_{n,j}(0)+o_{p}(1)=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{i})+o_{p}(1)

for each
j\in\{1,2,3\}
. Hence, by the central limit theorem,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\dagger}-\beta_{0})=\sum_{j=1}^{3}\frac{\sqrt{n}}{3}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{(j)}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\{\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})\}^{-1}}{i^{*}(p_{0})\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\sum_{i\in I_{j+2}}X_{i}\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{i})+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\{\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})\}^{-1}}{i^{*}(p_{0})\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\psi_{0}^{*}(\varepsilon_{i})+o_{p}(1)\overset{d}{\to}N_{d}\biggl(0,\frac{\{\mathbb{E}(X_{1}X_{1}^{\top})\}^{-1}}{i^{*}(p_{0})}\biggr)

as
n\to\infty
. By analogous reasoning based on Lemmas
32
and
33
,
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\beta}_{n}^{\ddagger}-\beta_{0})
has the same limiting distribution; note in particular that
R_{n}^{\ddagger}:=\sum_{j=1}^{3}R_{n,j}^{\ddagger}
satisfies
\sup_{\|{\beta}\|\in B_{M}}\|{R_{n}^{\ddagger}(b)-R_{n}^{\ddagger}(0)}\|=o_{p}(1)
.
∎

6.3.4
Proof of Theorem
14

For
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, let
\check{\psi}_{n,j}(\cdot):=\hat{\psi}_{n,j}(\cdot+\mu_{0})
and
\bar{\delta}_{n,j}:=\bar{X}_{n,j}^{\top}(\bar{\theta}_{n}^{(j)}-\theta_{0})
, and define
\tilde{W}_{i}:=\tilde{X}_{i}-\bar{X}_{n,j}
for
i\in I_{j+2}
. The pilot residuals in Step
3^{\prime}
can be written as
\hat{\varepsilon}_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}+\mu_{0}+X_{i}^{\top}b_{n}
for
i\in I_{j+1}
, where
b_{n}:=(\theta_{0}-\bar{\theta}_{n}^{(j)},0)=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
under the hypotheses of Theorem
14
, so the conclusions of Lemma
30
hold with
\mu=\mu_{0}
and
\hat{\Psi}_{n,j}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{\psi}_{n,j}(z-t)\,p_{0}(z-\mu_{0})\,dz=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\check{\psi}_{n,j}(z-t)\,p_{0}(z)\,dz
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
. In particular,
\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\check{\psi}_{n,j}-\psi_{0}^{*})^{2}\,p_{0}\overset{p}{\to}0
by (
82
).
