\hat{F}
is concave on
[0,1]
with
\hat{F}\geq F>-\infty
and
\hat{F}(v)\in\mathbb{R}
,
Rockafellar, 1997
ensures that
\hat{F}
is continuous at
v
. Therefore, because
F
is also continuous at
v
, there exists
\delta\in\bigl(0,\min(v,1-v)\bigr)
such that
\inf_{u\in[v-\delta,v+\delta]}\hat{F}(u)>\sup_{u\in[v-\delta,v+\delta]}F(u)
. Define
\ell\colon[0,1]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
to be the affine function that agrees with
\hat{F}
at
v-\delta
and
v+\delta
. Then
\ell>F
on
[v-\delta,v+\delta]
and moreover
\ell\geq\hat{F}\geq F
on
[0,1]\setminus[v-\delta,v+\delta]
, so
\ell\geq F
on
[0,1]
. It follows from the definition of the least concave majorant that
\hat{F}\wedge\ell=\hat{F}
and hence that
\hat{F}=\ell
on
[v-\delta,v+\delta]
, as required.
∎

Lemma 52
.

For a continuous function
F\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
, we have
\hat{F}(u)=F(u)
for
u\in\{0,1\}
and

\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\frac{F(u)-F(0)}{u}=\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}(0),\qquad\inf_{u\in(0,1)}\frac{F(1)-F(u)}{1-u}=\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{L})}(1).

Proof.

Let
C:=\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\bigl(F(u)-F(0)\bigr)/u
, so that
F(u)\leq F(0)+Cu
for all
u\in[0,1]
, where we adopt the convention
0\times\infty=0
. Since
\hat{F}
is the least concave majorant of
F
, we deduce that
\hat{F}(u)\leq F(0)+Cu
for all
u\in(0,1]
and
\hat{F}(0)=F(0)
. Thus,
\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\bigl(\hat{F}(u)-\hat{F}(0)\bigr)/u=C
. Moreover, by the concavity of
\hat{F}
and
Rockafellar, 1997
,

\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}(0)=\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}(u)\leq\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\frac{\int_{0}^{u}\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}}{u}=\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\frac{\hat{F}(u)-\hat{F}(0)}{u}\leq\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}(0),

so

\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\frac{F(u)-F(0)}{u}=\sup_{u\in(0,1)}\frac{\hat{F}(u)-\hat{F}(0)}{u}=\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}(0).

The remaining assertions of the lemma follow similarly by considering
u\mapsto F(1-u)
instead.
∎

Lemma 53
.

If
F\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
is differentiable at
v\in(0,1)
and its least concave majorant
\hat{F}
is finite at
v
, then
\hat{F}
is differentiable at
v
. Moreover,
\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{L})}
and
\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}
are both continuous (and coincide) at
v
.

Proof.

If
F(v)<\hat{F}(v)
, then by Lemma
51
,
\hat{F}
is affine on some open interval around
v
, so
\hat{F}
is differentiable at
v
in this case. On the other hand, if
F(v)=\hat{F}(v)
, then

\displaystyle\liminf_{u\nearrow v}\frac{F(v)-F(u)}{v-u}\geq\lim_{u\nearrow v}\frac{\hat{F}(v)-\hat{F}(u)}{v-u}

\displaystyle=\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{L})}(v)

\displaystyle\geq\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}(v)=\lim_{u\searrow v}\frac{\hat{F}(u)-\hat{F}(v)}{u-v}\geq\limsup_{u\searrow v}\frac{F(u)-F(v)}{u-v}.

Thus, if
F
is also differentiable at
v
, then
\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{L})}(v)=\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}(v)=F^{\prime}(v)
, and
Rockafellar, 1997
ensures that
\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{L})}
and
\hat{F}^{(\mathrm{R})}
are both continuous at
v
.
∎

Lemma 54
.

Given
0\leq u\leq v\leq 1
, suppose that
F\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
is convex on
[u,v]
, and concave on both
[0,u]
and
[v,1]
. Then there exist
u^{\prime}\in[0,u]
and
v^{\prime}\in[v,1]
such that the least concave majorant
\hat{F}
is affine on
[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]
and coincides with
F
on
[0,1]\setminus[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]
.

Proof.

If
u=v
, then the conclusion holds with
u^{\prime}=u=v=v^{\prime}
, so suppose now that
u<v
and define
\ell\colon[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
to be the affine function that agrees with
\hat{F}
at
u
and
v
. Then

\ell\geq\hat{F}\geq F
on
[0,1]\setminus[u,v]
by the concavity of
\hat{F}
, and moreover since
F
is convex on
[u,v]
, we have

F(w)\leq\frac{v-w}{v-u}\,F(u)+\frac{w-u}{v-u}\,F(v)\leq\frac{v-w}{v-u}\,\hat{F}(u)+\frac{w-u}{v-u}\,\hat{F}(v)=\ell(w)\leq\hat{F}(w)

for all
w\in[u,v]
. Therefore,
\ell\geq F
on
[0,1]
, so by the definition of the least concave majorant,
\hat{F}\wedge\ell=\hat{F}
. Let
u^{\prime}:=\inf\{w\in[0,u]:\hat{F}(w)=\ell(w)\}
and
v^{\prime}:=\sup\{w\in[v,1]:\hat{F}(w)=\ell(w)\}
. Then
\hat{F}=\ell
on
[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]
but
\hat{F}
is not locally affine at either
u^{\prime}
or
v^{\prime}
, so by Lemma
51
,
\hat{F}(w)=F(w)
for
w\in\{u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\}
. Since
F\leq\ell
is concave on both
[0,u^{\prime}]
and
[v^{\prime},1]
, we conclude that
\hat{F}=F
on
[0,1]\setminus[u^{\prime},v^{\prime}]
, as claimed.
∎

6.6
Additional simulation results

In Section
4
, we analysed the empirical performance of the
ASM
estimator without constraining the convex loss function to be symmetric. Here, we present simulation studies of
ASM
where we assume the noise distribution to be symmetric and hence enforce symmetry on the estimated loss function; see Section
3.3
. We generate data from the same linear model

Y_{i}=\mu_{0}+\tilde{X}_{i}^{\top}\theta_{0}+\varepsilon_{i}\quad\text{for $i\in[n]$}

as in Section
4
and take the noise distribution to be either (i) standard Gaussian, (ii) standard Cauchy, (iii) Gaussian scale mixture, (iv) Gaussian location mixture, or (v) smoothed uniform. We omit the smoothed exponential distribution since it is not symmetric. As the intercept
\mu_{0}
is now identifiable, we consider the estimation error of not just the coefficient
\theta_{0}
but also the intercept
\mu_{0}
, that is, we measure
\|\theta_{0}-\hat{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+(\mu_{0}-\hat{\mu})^{2}
.

We compare the
ASM
estimator to the same group of competitors that we use in Section
4
. To make the comparison fair, we modify the one-step estimator (1S) and the log-concave MLE (LCMLE) to also incorporate the symmetry assumption on the noise distribution.

Plots of the average squared error loss (
